# QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit

{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that the Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA) approaches the ground-state energy per spin of the Gaussian zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model when system size tends to infinity first and circuit depth then increases. For every accuracy, some finite depth and deterministic angles, independent of system size and disorder, achieve that accuracy in the limiting expected energy per spin using the standard cost Hamiltonian and transverse-field mixer. This proves the eventual Parisi-optimality conjecture of Basso, Farhi, Marwaha, Villalonga, and Zhou in its fixed-parameter thermodynamic formulation. We give no quantitative bound on the required depth or efficient angle-selection procedure.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA) approaches the ground-state energy per spin of the Gaussian zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model when system size tends to infinity first and circuit depth then increases. For every accuracy, some finite depth and deterministic angles, independent of system size and disorder, achieve that accuracy in the limiting expected energy per spin using the standard cost Hamiltonian and transverse-field mixer. This proves the eventual Parisi-optimality conjecture of Basso, Farhi, Marwaha, Villalonga, and Zhou in its fixed-parameter thermodynamic formulation. We give no quantitative bound on the required depth or efficient angle-selection procedure.

- **claim-thermodynamic-parisi-optimality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For the zero-field SK model <a href="#eq:sk-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sk-model">[eq:sk-model]</a>, $$\label{eq:main-result} \lim_{p\to\infty}Q_p=P_*.$$ Equivalently, for every $\varepsilon>0$ there exist a finite integer $p$ and deterministic real vectors $\gamma,\beta\in\mathbb R^p$, independent of $n$ and $J$, such that $v_p(\gamma,\beta)\ge P_*-\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: The supremum of the limiting expected QAOA energy approaches the SK optimum as depth increases. Each accuracy admits a finite word with deterministic angles independent of system size and disorder.
- **claim-regular-maxcut-successive-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-regular-maxcut)): For every $\eta>0$ there are an integer $p\ge1$, deterministic normalized angles $\gamma,\beta\in\mathbb R^p$, and an integer $D_0\ge2$ such that, for every integer $D\ge D_0$ and every finite simple unweighted $(D+1)$-regular graph $G$ of girth greater than $2p+1$, $$\label{eq:high-girth-maxcut} q_{p,G}(2\gamma/\sqrt D,\beta) \ge \frac12+\frac{P_*-\eta}{\sqrt D}.$$ The word $p,\gamma,\beta$ is independent of $D$, the graph size, and $G$; the displayed usual MaxCut cost angles, unlike the normalized angles $\gamma$, are scaled with the degree.* *Let $G_{n,d}$ be uniform among simple labelled $d$-regular graphs on $n$ vertices, along admissible $n$ with $nd$ even. For the same word, put $$\Delta_D(G)=\operatorname{OPT}(G) -q_{p,G}(2\gamma/\sqrt D,\beta).$$ The word may be chosen so that $$\label{eq:random-regular-maxcut} \lim_{D\to\infty} \limsup_{\substack{n\to\infty\\ n(D+1)\ \mathrm{even}}} \Pr_G\!\left[\sqrt D\,\Delta_D(G_{n,D+1})>\eta\right]=0.$$ Here the inner limit keeps $D,p,\gamma,\beta$ fixed, and $\Pr_G$ is only over the graph: $q_{p,G}$ already averages quantum measurement.
  - Explanation: A fixed normalized QAOA word gives the stated cut-fraction lower bound on sufficiently large-degree regular graphs with the required girth. Its comparison with the optimum is separately established for random regular graphs in successive limits.
- **claim-fixed-word-tree-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tree-transfer)): Let $W$ be an ordinary QAOA word, and apply the same word to the $(D+1)$-regular tree with cost $C_D=D^{-1/2}\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E}Z_iZ_j$, with each unordered edge counted once. Its expectation at an edge is defined by restricting to the finite light cone of that edge. Then $$\label{eq:tree-transfer} v_W=\lim_{D\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt D}{2} \langle W_D\boldsymbol\Omega\rvert Z_iZ_j\lvert W_D\boldsymbol\Omega\rangle.$$ Here $v_W$ is the disorder-averaged thermodynamic SK energy per vertex, with the normalization in the introduction. Both limits exist for every fixed word and every fixed choice of its angles.
  - Explanation: The thermodynamic SK value of an ordinary fixed word equals its limiting scaled edge correlation on the regular tree, with the positive-cost normalization.
- **claim-root-shift-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-energy)): Let $f_D$ be centered, root-invariant, finite-radius vectors on the $(D+1)$-regular tree. Root invariance means that the vector itself is fixed by every rooted permutation of complete child subtrees, including their seed coordinates, at every vertex of its finite neighborhood. Suppose their occupation embeddings converge strongly to $v$ at a fixed finite height and their expected top-generation occupations are bounded uniformly in $D$. Place the same rooted vector at the endpoints of an edge, calling the resulting vectors $f_{D,i}$ and $f_{D,j}$. Then $$\label{eq:energy-shift} \lim_{D\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt D}{2} \langle f_{D,i},f_{D,j}\rangle =\mathcal E(v):=\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\langle v,\mathsf Lv\rangle.$$ For any centered $v,w$, $$\label{eq:energy-continuity} \|\mathcal E(v)-\mathcal E(w)\| \leq(\\|v\\|+\\|w\\|)\\|v-w\\|.$$
  - Explanation: Strong occupation-embedded convergence together with a uniform occupation bound identifies the scaled edge pairing with the root-shift quadratic form. The same form is norm-continuous on centered vectors.
- **claim-weyl-weight-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weyl-bounds)): Let $N$ be the particle number on $\Gamma_s(H^\circ)$. For every integer $m\geq0$ there is $C_m$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|(1+N)^m\mathcal W(d)\xi\\| &\leq C_m(1+\\|d\\|)^{2m}\\|(1+N)^m\xi\\|, \label{eq:weyl-number-bound}\\ \\|(\mathcal W(d)-1)\xi\\| &\leq2\\|d\\|\\|(N+1)^{1/2}\xi\\|. \label{eq:weyl-small-bound} \end{aligned}$$ For the weight $W=W_0+\mathrm d\Gamma(\Lambda)$, the same conclusion holds with a polynomial in finitely many $\\|\Lambda^j d\\|$ in place of $(1+\\|d\\|)^{2m}$ and with a finite loss of input powers. All statements hold for spin-controlled displacements as well.
  - Explanation: Weyl displacements obey particle-number growth and small-displacement bounds. Recursive weights permit analogous estimates with weighted direction norms and a finite loss of input powers.
- **claim-centered-child-sums** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-centered-sums)): Let $T_D$ and $T_D^*$ have uniform smooth bounds on one-copy spaces, and suppose their smooth limits exist. If $\langle \boldsymbol\Omega,T_D\boldsymbol\Omega\rangle=0$, then under the occupation embeddings $$\label{eq:centered-sum-limit} D^{-1/2}\sum_{j=1}^D T_D^{(j)} \longrightarrow a^*(T\boldsymbol\Omega)+a(T^*\boldsymbol\Omega)$$ smoothly. Without centering, $D^{-1}\sum_jT_D^{(j)}\to\langle \boldsymbol\Omega,T\boldsymbol\Omega\rangle\,1$. Both families have uniform smooth bounds.
  - Explanation: Centered normalized child sums converge smoothly to creation and annihilation fields. Uncentered child averages instead converge to their vacuum mean.
- **claim-small-unitary-product-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-unitary-clt)): Let $K_D(x)$, $x\in\mathbb R^b$, be unitary, satisfy $K_D(0)=1$, and have uniform smooth bounds for all jets and adjoint jets. Suppose these jets converge smoothly and $\langle \boldsymbol\Omega,\partial_\alpha K_D(0)\boldsymbol\Omega\rangle=0$ for every first derivative. Write $$K_\alpha=\partial_\alpha K(0)=\mathrm iT_\alpha, \qquad K_{\alpha\lambda}=\partial_\alpha\partial_\lambda K(0).$$ The $T_\alpha$ are symmetric on the smooth domain. For every fixed $a\in\mathbb R^b$, $$\label{eq:unitary-product-limit} K_D(a/\sqrt D)^{\otimes D}\longrightarrow \exp\left\{\mathrm i\sum_\alpha a_\alpha q(T_\alpha\boldsymbol\Omega) +\frac12\sum_{\alpha,\lambda}a_\alpha a_\lambda \left\langle K_{\alpha\lambda} +\tfrac12\{T_\alpha,T_\lambda\}\right\rangle_0 \right\}.$$ The scalar in this exponent is imaginary. The convergence holds smoothly with all $a$-derivatives, locally uniformly in $a$, and the product has polynomial smooth bounds in $a$, uniform in $D$. The assertion also holds with an additional finite list of unscaled parameters $\theta$: use $K_D(a/\sqrt D;\theta)^{\otimes D}$, assume $K_D(0;\theta)=1$ and first-derivative centering for every $\theta$, and assume the hypotheses jointly for all parameter jets, uniformly on compact $\theta$-sets. The conclusion then includes all $\theta$-jets and the same compact uniformity.
  - Explanation: Products of small child unitaries converge to a field exponential with an imaginary scalar correction determined by their second derivatives. Joint parameter and adjoint convergence are retained.
- **claim-ordinary-root-update-calculus** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-updates)): Ordinary finite words have smooth root limits and smooth adjoint limits at every sufficient finite height. Starting at $u=1$, the limiting left updates for a mixer of angle $\beta$ and a cost of angle $t$ are, respectively, $$\label{eq:ordinary-root-updates} u\longmapsto e^{-\mathrm i\beta X}u, \qquad u\longmapsto e^{-\mathrm itZq(v_Z)}u.$$ The cost update leaves $v_Z$ unchanged. For a cost conjugated by a global mixer, replace $Z$ by the same rotated Pauli $P$ both at the root and in $v_P$. A Gaussian seed gate has the additional update $u\mapsto e^{-\mathrm itZs}u$.* *All these assertions are unchanged when the top root has $D+1$ children and the descendants have $D$ children, still using the coefficient $D^{-1/2}$. For an ordinary word $W$, its limiting insertion $v_Z$ therefore satisfies $v_W=\mathcal E(v_Z)$, with $\mathcal E$ defined in Proposition <a href="#prop:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:energy">4</a>.
  - Explanation: Ordinary words admit smooth limiting root updates for mixers and costs. Seeded gates have an additional local update, and the ordinary insertion energy agrees with the transferred SK value.
- **claim-commuting-control-substitution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-commuting-substitution)): Let $S_D=(S_{D,1},\ldots,S_{D,b})$ be a fixed finite list of strongly commuting self-adjoint operators. Suppose their polynomials converge smoothly and their joint exponentials satisfy, for each $m$, bounds $$\label{eq:joint-exponential-bound} \\|W_D^m e^{\mathrm it\cdot S_D}\xi\\| \leq C_m(1+\|t\|)^{k_m}\\|W_D^{r_m}\xi\\|,$$ uniformly in $D,t$, and converge strongly on smooth inputs. Let $F_D(x)$ and $F_D(x)^*$ have uniform smooth bounds and converge with all their jets on compact $x$-sets, and suppose $F_D(x)$ commutes with the spectral projections of every $S_{D,j}$. Then the substitution $F_D(S_D)$ has uniform smooth bounds and converges smoothly. The assertion includes extra scalar parameters and their jets. If $F_D(x)$ is unitary for real $x$, so is $F_D(S_D)$. The limiting list $S$ consists of the strongly commuting self-adjoint generators of the limiting joint unitary group. Their actions and polynomials on the smooth domain agree with the assumed polynomial limits.* *For a real smooth function $f(s,x)$ whose derivatives have polynomial growth in $x$, uniformly in $s$, the operators $e^{\mathrm it f(s,S_D)}$ have polynomial smooth bounds in $t$. Polynomial growth in a Gaussian $s$ is also permitted with the seed-moment weight. In every seed-dependent assertion, the local seed multiplication algebra is required to strongly commute with every $S_{D,j}$ and with the operator coefficient families; thus $f(s,S_D)$ is a joint commuting substitution. No nondegeneracy assumption on the joint covariance of the $S_{D,j}$ is needed.
  - Explanation: Joint commuting functional calculus preserves smooth convergence, bounds and unitarity when the signal list and operator coefficients satisfy the stated commutation and convergence conditions. Degenerate covariance is permitted.
- **claim-smooth-exclusion-gate-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-gate-calculus)): Fix a finite sequence of exclusion gates, ordinary mixers and costs, and local seed gates. Its root interaction-picture maps, their adjoints, and their root insertion vectors have smooth limits under the occupation embeddings. They have uniform smooth bounds at every sufficient finite height. The same limit is obtained at a full root with $D+1$ children. Hence their centered insertion energies are given by Proposition <a href="#prop:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:energy">4</a>.* *An exclusion gate may be replaced inside any such fixed sequence by a sequence of pointwise unitary matrices with the same parameter calculations, provided every fixed number of aggregate-input derivatives converges on compact sets and has uniform polynomial bounds. Convergence almost everywhere in the local seed, or convergence in seed measure followed by a subsequence, is sufficient for bounded measurable local-seed coefficients. The resulting root maps, adjoints, and insertion vectors converge smoothly, and their energies converge.* *If local-seed coefficients are uniformly bounded, these bounds hold conditionally on the root seed, with constants independent of its value, using $W_0=1$. Root maps commute with root-seed multiplication. Consequently, if a root column $c$ has conditional $m$th graph norm at most $C_m$, then $$\label{eq:masked-column-bound} \\|W^m\mathbf 1_Jc\\|\leq C_m\sqrt{\mu(J)}.$$ The same statements hold for any further scalar boundary-input jets of a fixed exclusion calculation. They also hold, including adjoint jets, for a finite list of external unscaled parameters, locally uniformly in those parameters, when the matrix and signal hypotheses hold jointly for their mixed jets.
  - Explanation: Fixed exclusion-gate sequences have smooth limiting maps, adjoints and insertions. Admissible matrix approximations remain valid inside a fixed surrounding sequence, including conditional bounds on masked root columns.
- **claim-additive-cut-response** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-additive-response)): Fix a preceding finite sequence as in Theorem <a href="#thm:gate-calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:gate-calculus">14</a>. Suppose a temporary Hamiltonian is diagonal in a common collection of site axes, with coefficients given by an exclusion calculation. Require its local Hamiltonian matrices and every aggregate-input derivative to have polynomial bounds as in Definition <a href="#def:exclusion-gate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:exclusion-gate">13</a>. In a cut branch let $H_D(a)$ denote the sum of the Hamiltonian terms affected by scalar boundary input $a$, truncated beyond its finite influence distance and conjugated by the preceding subtree circuit. Assume explicitly that $$\label{eq:additive-response-parity} H_D(-a)=\mathcal{P} H_D(a)\mathcal{P}, \qquad \mathcal{P}\boldsymbol\Omega=\boldsymbol\Omega,$$ where $\mathcal{P}$ is the joint spin/seed symmetry. The first two boundary derivatives have smooth limits $H_\alpha=\lim_D\partial_\alpha H_D(0)$ and $H_{\alpha\lambda}=\lim_D\partial_\alpha\partial_\lambda H_D(0)$, and $H_\alpha$ is centered. These limits concern the response derivatives; no limit for the extensive $H_D(0)$ is required. The change in its $D$ child branches when zero boundary input is replaced by $a/\sqrt D$ is $$\sum_{j=1}^D \bigl[H_D(a/\sqrt D)-H_D(0)\bigr]^{(j)}.$$ Its smooth limit is $$\label{eq:additive-cut-response} \sum_\alpha a_\alpha q(H_\alpha\boldsymbol\Omega) +\frac12\sum_{\alpha,\lambda}a_\alpha a_\lambda \langle H_{\alpha\lambda}\rangle_0.$$ The correction is realized as the self-adjoint generator of the limiting time group of the child cut ratios; the display specifies its action on the smooth domain. We use the same realization after commuting substitution and for the correction generators below. The same formula applies recursively to lower cut responses and to their input jets. It is continuous with the smooth bounds of Theorem <a href="#thm:gate-calculus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:gate-calculus">14</a>, for a fixed finite list of channels. Commuting parameter operators may replace $a$ under the same exclusions.* *In particular, if a sequence of such correction generators $R_j$ satisfies $\\|R_j\xi\\|\leq\delta_j\\|W^r\xi\\|$ with $\delta_j\to0$, then $$\label{eq:small-generator-error} \\|(e^{\mathrm itR_j}-1)\xi\\| \leq \|t\|\delta_j\\|W^r\xi\\|.$$ If their exponentials and adjoints also have uniform smooth bounds, these errors tend to zero in every fixed lower graph norm and may be inserted in a fixed surrounding sequence.
  - Explanation: The limiting Hamiltonian response across a cut contains both a centered first-derivative field and a scalar second-derivative correction. The statement concerns response derivatives rather than an extensive baseline Hamiltonian.
- **claim-rooted-gaussian-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-rooted-realization)): Fix $T,N$ and the scalar data in <a href="#eq:scalar-euler" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-euler">[eq:scalar-euler]</a>–<a href="#eq:scalar-coefficients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-coefficients">[eq:scalar-coefficients]</a>, with all $c_j$ finite and positive. There is a real odd finite descendant formula $F_N$ of height at most $N+1$ in the classical rooted space whose Gaussian coordinates have exactly the stated joint law and whose energy satisfies $$\mathcal E(F_N)=\sum_{j=0}^N A_j B_{j+1}. \label{eq:scalar-rooted-energy}$$ The formula is bounded by one, although its transmitted columns need not be bounded.
  - Explanation: An orthonormal child-direction induction realizes the finite scalar Gaussian law exactly in the rooted space. Its first-chaos component yields the adjacent-index coefficient formula for energy.
- **claim-bounded-trigonometric-density** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-classical-trigonometric-density)): Let $m^*=\pi(z)$, where $z$ is a finite descendant formula with Gaussian leaf seeds. Assume that every transmitted child column is odd, and that each local map producing such a column or the output is a jointly odd smooth function of its local seed and finite list of odd aggregate inputs, with polynomial-growth derivatives. Purely local intermediate operations may be composed into these maps before the lemma is applied. For every $\eta>0$, there is an odd bounded angle formula $\theta$ such that $$\lVert \sin(2\theta)-m^*\rVert<\eta. \label{eq:angle-density}$$ Every sent column in the formula for $\theta$ can be chosen bounded and odd. Its pointwise maps can be chosen to be finite real trigonometric sums in their finite lists of odd inputs, with odd joint parity for odd outputs. Its local seed inputs can be chosen bounded and odd. All these functions have bounded derivatives of every order in their continuous arguments.
  - Explanation: Odd smooth descendant formulas with polynomial-growth derivatives admit bounded odd angle approximations. Transmitted columns, seed inputs and pointwise trigonometric maps can all be made bounded.
- **claim-color-exclusions-small-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-classical-color-exclusion)): Fix a descendant formula in which every transmitted column and the output are given by jointly odd bounded smooth functions of the local odd seed and the odd aggregate inputs, with bounded first derivatives. Let its propagation depth be $h$. It has a finite realization by colored formulas such that every aggregation excludes the current color, all colors of the path ancestors, and all helper colors. If $f$ is its original output and $f^{\mathrm{col}}$ its colored output, set to zero on helper roots, then $$\lVert f^{\mathrm{col}}-f\rVert^2\le C(\alpha+p_H), \label{eq:color-exclusion-bound}$$ where $C$ depends only on the fixed color-free formula, not on the number or individual probabilities of its nonhelper colors. Conditional bounds for the unchanged nonlinear pointwise functions and their derivatives in the original grouped aggregate coordinates are uniform over these colors. No bound independent of $M$ is asserted for the Euclidean derivative norm after flattening all source-color fields into separate coordinates.
  - Explanation: Deleting current, ancestor and helper colors perturbs a fixed bounded smooth formula by a controlled norm error. The bound is uniform in the color partition for grouped aggregate coordinates, rather than flattened color coordinates.
- **claim-classical-near-optimal-exclusion-formula** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-classical-near-optimum)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ and every $\eta_H>0$, there are a finite color partition with helper probability $0<p_H<\eta_H$, a finite number of helper types, and finite bounded odd angle formulas $\theta_c$ on the nonhelper colors such that the following hold. Each transmitted column is bounded and odd, every pointwise map may be chosen as a finite trigonometric sum in its odd inputs, and every aggregation excludes its current color, all path-ancestor colors, and all helpers. Conditional on the finite labels, all continuous derivatives of the pointwise maps are bounded. The output $$m^{\mathrm{col}} =\sum_{c=1}^M\mathbf 1_{\{C=c\}}\sin(2\theta_c) \label{eq:final-classical-formula}$$ is centered, satisfies $\|m^{\mathrm{col}}\|\le1$, vanishes on helper roots, and obeys $$\mathcal E(m^{\mathrm{col}})\ge P_*-\varepsilon. \label{eq:final-classical-value}$$ After the uncolored finite formula has been fixed, the total helper probability may be taken arbitrarily small, with all individual helper probabilities positive. All depths, coefficients, label laws, and functions in the conclusion are fixed independently of degree, system size, and disorder.
  - Explanation: The scalar route, bounded approximation and type exclusions produce a finite centered trigonometric output near the SK optimum. Helper probabilities are positive and may be chosen arbitrarily small after fixing the uncolored formula.
- **claim-seeded-classical-synthesis** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-classical-synthesis)): For every finite collection of bounded smooth odd seed-only exclusion formulas of Definition <a href="#def:exclusion-formulas" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:exclusion-formulas">19</a>, with disjoint positive-probability helper masks, one for each positive aggregation depth, absent from all their dependencies and with output angle zero on their union $H$, and every $\eta>0$, there is a finite seeded circuit whose insertion vector $v$ satisfies $$\lVert \mathbf 1_{H^c}(v-v_{\mathrm{cl}})\rVert<\eta, \label{eq:synthesis-insertion}$$ where $v_{\mathrm{cl}}$ is the insertion vector of <a href="#eq:ideal-classical-rotations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ideal-classical-rotations">[eq:ideal-classical-rotations]</a>. In particular its value obeys $$\lvert \mathcal E(v)-\mathcal E(m)\rvert\le 2\eta+2\sqrt{p_H}. \label{eq:synthesis-energy}$$ The circuit and all its angles are chosen independently of the degree, system size, and disorder. Helper probabilities can be arbitrarily small but are fixed and positive when the construction is performed.
  - Explanation: Finite seeded circuits approximate the ideal classical insertion off helper roots. The energy error also includes the cost of discarding helper output, with all circuit parameters fixed independently of degree and disorder.
- **claim-simultaneous-helper-contraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-helper-contraction)): Consider a fixed finite sequence in the parity-preserving, root-seed-commuting gate-calculus class, containing finitely many propagations <a href="#eq:desired-propagation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:desired-propagation">[eq:desired-propagation]</a>, possibly with different $I,J,f,P$, and otherwise fixed gates. All its gates and classical parameters avoid a common helper $K$. Start with its spins pinned at $X=1$. Replace every indicated propagation by <a href="#eq:helper-contraction-generator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:helper-contraction-generator">[eq:helper-contraction-generator]</a>, and refine their sender partitions so that their largest part probability tends to zero. Then all insertion vectors off $K$ converge to those of the original sequence. The root maps and their adjoints have uniform conditional smooth bounds on the compressed space, and converge strongly there up to scalar phases depending only on the root label. The same estimates hold on cut subtrees. Responses through the fixed lower formulas for $f$ have the uniform jet estimates proved below; no convergence of arbitrary boundary jets in the growing list of partition-dependent top channels is asserted.
  - Explanation: Refining sender partitions makes helper-mediated propagations approximate the original fixed sequence off the pinned helper mask. Root maps converge up to local seed phases, while lower-formula response estimates retain their fixed channel scope.
- **claim-fixed-commuting-field-exponentials** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-field-exponentials)): Let $Q_j=q(d_j)$, $1\le j\le k$, commute, where $k$ is fixed and $\lVert d_j\rVert_a\le C_a$ for every $a$. Let $F_s:\mathbb R^k\to\mathbb R$ have polynomial bounds on every derivative, uniformly in the local seed $s$. For bounded $t$, the operators $\exp(\mathrm itF_s(Q))$, their adjoints, and their time derivatives are uniformly smooth. Auxiliary parameter jets obey the same conclusion when the weighted column jets and the joint derivatives of $F$ have the corresponding uniform polynomial bounds. The constants require no lower bound on the Gram matrix of the $d_j$’s. The same assertion holds with a fixed root control axis, by conditioning on its two eigenvalues.
  - Explanation: Smooth functions of a fixed commuting field list have uniformly smooth exponentials, adjoints and parameter derivatives under weighted upper bounds. No inverse covariance bound is required.
- **claim-orthogonal-quadratic-smallness** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-orthogonal-quadratics)): Let $R_l$ be finitely many pairwise disjoint root masks, with $p_l=\Pr(R_l)$. Suppose $d_l,e_l$ are supported on $R_l$ and are real-commuting, meaning that all fields $q(d_l),q(e_l)$ commute pairwise. Suppose also that for every $a$ $$\lVert d_l\rVert_a+\lVert e_l\rVert_a\le C_a\sqrt{p_l}.$$ Set $$Q=\sum_l\left(q(d_l)q(e_l)-\langle d_l,e_l\rangle\right), \qquad \rho=\left(\sum_l p_l^2\right)^{1/2}.$$ Then $Q=O_{\mathrm{sm}}(\rho)$. Its exponentials and adjoints are uniformly smooth for bounded times, independently of the number of columns. They converge to the identity strongly on smooth vectors as $\rho\to0$, and in every fixed lower smooth norm on families with all higher smooth norms bounded.
  - Explanation: Disjoint root masks control the combined quadratic kernels by the squared mask probabilities. This gives small generators and uniform exponential bounds independent of the number of columns.
- **claim-removal-of-gaussian-seeds** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-seed-simulation)): For every finite seeded word and every $\epsilon>0$, there is a finite ordinary cost–mixer word whose limiting tree energy differs from that of the seeded word by less than $\epsilon$. Its angles are independent of the degree, the system size, and the disorder. In particular, the approximation uses the thermodynamic limit at a fixed word.
  - Explanation: A fixed finite seeded word can be approximated in limiting tree energy using only ordinary costs and mixers. The entire approximating word is selected before taking the thermodynamic limit.
- **claim-displaced-packet-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-packet-estimates)): The following estimates are uniform for bounded families of tails.* 1. *Displacements with bounded directions preserve all the seminorms $\lVert \cdot\rVert_k$, with bounds depending only on the direction norm and $k$. For $\lVert d\rVert\leq C$, $$W(rd)\eta=\eta+\mathrm ir q(d)\eta+O(r^2)\lVert \eta\rVert_1 \quad\hbox{in norm}.$$ If $d_r-e_r\to0$ in norm and both directions are bounded, then $(W(d_r)-W(e_r))\eta_r\to0$ in norm. These assertions also hold for $W_P$.* 2. *If $\lVert d_r-e_r\rVert\geq\kappa>0$, then for every integer $M$, $$\label{eq:seed-packet-separation} \lvert \langle W(d_r/r)\eta_r,W(e_r/r)\xi_r\rangle\rvert=O(r^M).$$ The conclusion is unchanged by inserting a bounded root spin matrix.* 3. *For bounded $d_r,v_r$ and fixed $\tau$, conjugating the short probe $e^{-\mathrm ir\tau Pq(v_r)}$ through a packet gives $$\label{eq:seed-packet-probe} W(d_r/r)^*e^{-\mathrm ir\tau Pq(v_r)}W(d_r/r) =e^{-\mathrm i\tau a_rP}e^{-\mathrm ir\tau Pq(v_r)}, \qquad a_r=-2\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\langle v_r,d_r\rangle.$$ Consequently a fixed finite list of short probes preserves its scaled centers and bounded centered-tail moments, without any smallness assumption on $a_r$.
  - Explanation: Bounded displacements preserve tail moments, separated packets have rapidly vanishing overlap, and short probes retain packet centers while producing spin rotations. Norm remainders are handled separately from moment-controlled tails.
- **claim-initial-high-direction-decoupling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-high-directions)): The vectors $\ell_r,w_r$ are centered, have norm one, and satisfy $$\langle \ell_r,w_r\rangle\longrightarrow0, \qquad \lVert \mathbf 1_{N\leq M}\ell_r\rVert+ \lVert \mathbf 1_{N\leq M}w_r\rVert\longrightarrow0 \quad\hbox{for every fixed }M.$$ They consequently have vanishing inner products with every bounded family of top-number-smooth vectors.
  - Explanation: The initial high directions are centered and normalized, become mutually orthogonal, and escape every fixed occupation sector. They therefore decouple from bounded top-number-smooth families.
- **claim-exact-zero-clock-ensemble-control** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-ensemble-control)): Let $h_1,\ldots,h_m$ be a finite list of nonzero vectors in $\mathbb R^2$. There are two perpendicular axes $P_1,P_2$ in the $ZY$-plane with the following property. Set $c_{j,a}=h_j\cdot P_a$, using the coordinates $(Z,Y)$. Given any $\theta\in\mathbb R$, there is a finite list $(a_k,\tau_k)$, with $a_k\in\{1,2\}$, such that $$\label{eq:seed-zero-clocks} \sum_{k:a_k=a}\tau_k=0\quad(a=1,2), \qquad \prod_k e^{-\mathrm i\tau_k c_{j,a_k}P_{a_k}} =e^{-\mathrm i\theta X}\quad(1\leq j\leq m).$$ The product is in chronological order, with later factors on the left. The assertion is exact, not merely an assertion about closure.
  - Explanation: A finite ensemble of nonzero projection vectors admits an exact common spin rotation using perpendicular axes and pulse lists whose clocks cancel separately. The reachability argument proves finite products rather than closure alone.
- **claim-selective-probe-accuracy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-selective-accuracy)): Consider a stage whose vacuum state $u_r\boldsymbol\Omega$ has a finite packet expansion, with norm-$o(r)$ remainders and uniformly bounded scaled centers. Suppose the ordinary component has order-one amplitude and the special component has amplitude $O(r)$. All designated tails have bounded top-number moments. Suppose also that one of the following ordinary geometries holds.* 1. *At a standard stage all ordinary centers are zero.* 2. *At a huge-cost stage the ordinary part is $\psi_++\psi_-$, with $Z\psi_y=y\psi_y$ and centers $-tyv_r$, where $t\neq0$ and $v_r=u_r^*Zu_r\boldsymbol\Omega$. Every special center $d_{j,r}$ obeys $\lim\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\langle v_r,d_{j,r}\rangle\neq0$.* *Assume the limiting special projection pairs $$h_j=\lim_{r\downarrow0}-2 \bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\langle v_{Z,r},d_{j,r}\rangle, \mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\langle v_{Y,r},d_{j,r}\rangle\bigr)$$ exist and are nonzero. Use the axes and finite pulse list of Lemma <a href="#lem:seed-ensemble-control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:seed-ensemble-control">29</a> for these pairs, replacing its spin generators by actual toggled probes of angles $r\tau_k$. Then the net probe action is the identity up to $o(r)$ on every ordinary packet, and is the prescribed $X$-rotation up to $o(1)$ on every normalized special packet. The ordinary assertion holds also after any root spin matrix has been inserted in its tail. These statements hold uniformly for bounded families of tails sharing the stated center list, with the probes chosen solely from the vacuum data.
  - Explanation: Vacuum-selected probes cancel their leading action on ordinary packets and implement the desired rotation on special packets. The uniform estimates also apply to bounded tail families with the same center list and to spin-inserted ordinary tails.
- **claim-low-insertion-forward-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-low-insertions)): If <a href="#eq:seed-exact-special-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:seed-exact-special-norm">[eq:seed-exact-special-norm]</a> and <a href="#eq:seed-low-ordinary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:seed-low-ordinary">[eq:seed-low-ordinary]</a> hold uniformly on bounded input families, then $$\label{eq:seed-insertion-O-r} \lVert u_r^*Pu_r\boldsymbol\Omega-A_r^*PA_r\boldsymbol\Omega\rVert=O(r) \qquad(P=Y,Z).$$ In particular the actual insertion directions have bounded smooth approximants. No approximation to $u_r^*$ on a high packet is needed.
  - Explanation: Uniform forward approximation and the exact small-component norm imply a norm estimate for actual insertions against their smooth low approximants. An adjoint approximation on a high packet is unnecessary.
- **claim-singular-echo-invariant** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-one-echo)): Under the invariant and the visibility conditions, the echo <a href="#eq:seed-echo-list" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:seed-echo-list">[eq:seed-echo-list]</a> preserves the invariant. Up to a scalar of modulus one its low update is $$\label{eq:seed-echo-low} A_r\longmapsto W_Z(g_r)A_r, \qquad g_r=\frac{t}{r}(v'_r-v_r)=-2t w_r+o(1),$$ where $v_r$ is the actual $Z$-insertion before the forward cost and $v'_r$ is the actual insertion immediately after the selective flip. The special-center update can be taken to be exactly $$\label{eq:seed-D-update} D_r\longmapsto D_r-t(v_r+v'_r).$$ All ordinary errors are $o(r)$ before and after the large costs.
  - Explanation: The echo preserves the packet invariant and converts the actual insertion difference into a bounded longitudinal-field direction. Exact cancellation precedes singular rescaling, so ordinary norm errors remain controlled through the large costs.
- **claim-finite-gram-and-energy-induction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-gram-induction)): Suppose the required selective probe lists have been constructed through a given standard stage. The joint Gram matrix of $e$, all actual standard-stage insertion vectors used so far, and $\ell_r,w_r$, converges to that of the corresponding formal endogenous directions and the two orthogonal external directions. The limiting energy of the actual insertion converges to the energy of the formal seeded insertion. The same assertions hold at the standard ordinary geometry just before the second flip in an echo, with the temporary transport sign retained.
  - Explanation: Finite Weyl expansions identify the limiting Gram matrix with orthogonal external and endogenous directions. A separate first-particle root-shift calculation proves energy convergence as well.
- **claim-huge-cost-transverse-decoupling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-seed-huge-transverse)): Immediately after a forward cost with fixed $t\neq0$, the actual $Y$-insertion has vanishing pairing with every bounded family in $\mathcal S$. In particular, its pairing with every special scaled center tends to zero.
  - Explanation: A forward huge cost separates the opposite ordinary packets required by a transverse spin insertion. Its pairing with bounded smooth families and special scaled centers consequently vanishes.
- **claim-positive-temperature-value-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-temperature-bound)): For every $\beta_0>1$ and $0<T<q$, $$\int_0^T\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\,\mathrm dt \ge P_* -\frac{1/4+\log2}{\beta_0}-(q-T). \label{eq:positive-temperature-bound}$$ Moreover, $$\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)^2=1\quad(0\le t\le q), \qquad 1-q\le\beta_0^{-1}. \label{eq:parisi-normalization-and-endpoint}$$
  - Explanation: Positive-temperature self-consistency yields curvature normalization and an endpoint bound. The integrated curvature approximates the ground-state optimum with explicit temperature and cutoff losses.
- **claim-finite-step-gaussian-edge-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-eams-finite-step)): For the preceding construction, as $D\to\infty$ at fixed $\delta,L$, $$\begin{aligned} c_{D,r}&\longrightarrow c_{\delta,r}, \qquad 0\le r\le L, \label{eq:eams-normalizer-limit}\\ (u^0_i,\ldots,u^L_i)&\Longrightarrow(U_0,\ldots,U_L), \qquad z_i^L\Longrightarrow Z^\delta_L, \label{eq:eams-distribution-limit}\\ \lim_{D\to\infty}\frac{D+1}{2\sqrt D}\mathbb Ez_o^Lz_v^L &=\delta\sum_{r=1}^{L-1} c_{\delta,r}\mathbb Ea(t_r,X^\delta_r), \qquad o\sim v. \label{eq:eams-corrected-value} \end{aligned}$$ The sum in <a href="#eq:eams-corrected-value" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:eams-corrected-value">[eq:eams-corrected-value]</a> is empty when $L=1$. The distributional statements include convergence of every fixed moment. The analogous joint Gaussian limit holds for a directed edge. Moreover $\mathbb E(Z^\delta_L)^2=L\delta$.
  - Explanation: At fixed mesh and horizon, the tree messages, normalizers and output moments converge to their Gaussian counterparts. The edge value uses the adjacent-index finite sum, which is empty at the shortest horizon.
- **claim-fixed-temperature-continuum-errors** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-eams-positive-continuum)): Fix $\beta_0>1$ and $0<T<q$, and let $L=\lfloor T/\delta\rfloor$. There is a coupling with the diffusion <a href="#eq:positive-parisi-diffusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-parisi-diffusion">[eq:positive-parisi-diffusion]</a> and a constant $C=C(\beta_0,T)$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\delta$, $$\begin{aligned} \max_{r\delta\le T}\mathbb E\|X^\delta_r-X_{r\delta}\|^2&\le C\delta, &\max_{r\delta\le T}\|c_{\delta,r}-1\|&\le C\sqrt\delta, \label{eq:euler-normalization-bound}\\ \mathbb E\|Z^\delta_L-m(T,X_T)\|^2&\le C\delta, \label{eq:martingale-euler-bound}\\ \left\|\delta\sum_{r=1}^{L-1}c_{\delta,r}\mathbb Ea(t_r,X^\delta_r) -\int_0^T\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\,\mathrm dt\right\| &\le C\sqrt\delta. \label{eq:value-riemann-bound} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: At fixed temperature and cutoff, a diffusion coupling controls the Euler state, normalizers, martingale output and energy Riemann sum as the mesh shrinks.
- **claim-strong-symmetric-power-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-classical-strong-limit)): Consider a fixed finite descendant formula whose pointwise functions are smooth with all derivatives of polynomial growth. Assume its leaf seeds have moments of every order and every aggregated child column has mean zero under its finite-degree child law for each $D$. Its finite-degree root vector converges in norm, under the symmetric occupation embeddings, to the formula obtained by replacing every normalized sum by $\mathsf G$. The same conclusion holds for a convergent finite collection of deterministic coefficients in the formula. The finite-degree root vectors have a uniformly bounded first child-occupation moment. Consequently, for centered equivariant outputs $f_D$ and their limit $f$, $$\lim_{D\to\infty}\frac{D+1}{2\sqrt D}\mathbb Ef_D(o)f_D(v) =\mathcal E(f),\qquad o\sim v. \label{eq:classical-energy-transfer}$$
  - Explanation: Smooth polynomial-growth descendant formulas with centered child columns converge in norm under occupation embeddings. A separate child-replacement argument bounds occupation and allows transfer of centered equivariant edge energies.
- **claim-positive-temperature-near-optimal-formula** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-classical-near-optimum-positive-temperature)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ and every $\eta_H>0$, there are a finite color partition with helper probability $0<p_H<\eta_H$, a finite number of helper types, and finite bounded odd angle formulas $\theta_c$ on the nonhelper colors such that the following hold. Each transmitted column is bounded and odd, every pointwise map may be chosen as a finite trigonometric sum in its odd inputs, and every aggregation excludes its current color, all path-ancestor colors, and all helpers. Conditional on the finite labels, all continuous derivatives of the pointwise maps are bounded. The output $$m^{\mathrm{col}} =\sum_{c=1}^M\mathbf 1_{\{C=c\}}\sin(2\theta_c) \label{eq:positive-final-classical-formula}$$ is centered, satisfies $\|m^{\mathrm{col}}\|\le1$, vanishes on helper roots, and obeys $$\mathcal E(m^{\mathrm{col}})\ge P_*-\varepsilon. \label{eq:positive-final-classical-value}$$ After the uncolored finite formula has been fixed, the total helper probability may be taken arbitrarily small, with all individual helper probabilities positive. All depths, coefficients, label laws, and functions in the conclusion are fixed independently of degree, system size, and disorder.
  - Explanation: The independent positive-temperature route produces the same bounded odd trigonometric exclusion interface as the primary route. All formula data and positive helper probabilities are fixed after the finite calculation's degree limit.

        "exact_quote": "Let $f_D$ be centered, root-invariant, finite-radius vectors on the $(D+1)$-regular tree.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-weyl-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be the particle number on $\\Gamma_s(H^\\circ)$. For every integer $m\\geq0$ there is $C_m$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|(1+N)^m\\mathcal W(d)\\xi\\|\n &\\leq C_m(1+\\|d\\|)^{2m}\\|(1+N)^m\\xi\\|,\n       \\label{eq:weyl-number-bound}\\\\\n \\|(\\mathcal W(d)-1)\\xi\\|\n &\\leq2\\|d\\|\\|(N+1)^{1/2}\\xi\\|.\n       \\label{eq:weyl-small-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For the weight $W=W_0+\\mathrm d\\Gamma(\\Lambda)$, the same conclusion holds with a polynomial in finitely many $\\|\\Lambda^j d\\|$ in place of $(1+\\|d\\|)^{2m}$ and with a finite loss of input powers. All statements hold for spin-controlled displacements as well.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-centered-sums",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T_D$ and $T_D^*$ have uniform smooth bounds on one-copy spaces, and suppose their smooth limits exist. If $\\langle \\boldsymbol\\Omega,T_D\\boldsymbol\\Omega\\rangle=0$, then under the occupation embeddings $$\\label{eq:centered-sum-limit}\n D^{-1/2}\\sum_{j=1}^D T_D^{(j)}\n \\longrightarrow a^*(T\\boldsymbol\\Omega)+a(T^*\\boldsymbol\\Omega)$$ smoothly.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-unitary-clt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K_D(x)$, $x\\in\\mathbb R^b$, be unitary, satisfy $K_D(0)=1$, and have uniform smooth bounds for all jets and adjoint jets. Suppose these jets converge smoothly and $\\langle \\boldsymbol\\Omega,\\partial_\\alpha K_D(0)\\boldsymbol\\Omega\\rangle=0$ for every first derivative. Write $$K_\\alpha=\\partial_\\alpha K(0)=\\mathrm iT_\\alpha,\n \\qquad K_{\\alpha\\lambda}=\\partial_\\alpha\\partial_\\lambda K(0).$$ The $T_\\alpha$ are symmetric on the smooth domain. For every fixed $a\\in\\mathbb R^b$, $$\\label{eq:unitary-product-limit}\n K_D(a/\\sqrt D)^{\\otimes D}\\longrightarrow\n \\exp\\left\\{\\mathrm i\\sum_\\alpha a_\\alpha q(T_\\alpha\\boldsymbol\\Omega)\n +\\frac12\\sum_{\\alpha,\\lambda}a_\\alpha a_\\lambda\n \\left\\langle K_{\\alpha\\lambda}\n           +\\tfrac12\\{T_\\alpha,T_\\lambda\\}\\right\\rangle_0\n \\right\\}.$$ The scalar in this exponent is imaginary. The convergence holds smoothly with all $a$-derivatives, locally uniformly in $a$, and the product has polynomial smooth bounds in $a$, uniform in $D$. The assertion also holds with an additional finite list of unscaled parameters $\\theta$: use $K_D(a/\\sqrt D;\\theta)^{\\otimes D}$, assume $K_D(0;\\theta)=1$ and first-derivative centering for every $\\theta$, and assume the hypotheses jointly for all parameter jets, uniformly on compact $\\theta$-sets. The conclusion then includes all $\\theta$-jets and the same compact uniformity.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-updates",
        "exact_quote": "Ordinary finite words have smooth root limits and smooth adjoint limits at every sufficient finite height. Starting at $u=1$, the limiting left updates for a mixer of angle $\\beta$ and a cost of angle $t$ are, respectively, $$\\label{eq:ordinary-root-updates}\n u\\longmapsto e^{-\\mathrm i\\beta X}u,\n \\qquad\n u\\longmapsto e^{-\\mathrm itZq(v_Z)}u.$$ The cost update leaves $v_Z$ unchanged. For a cost conjugated by a global mixer, replace $Z$ by the same rotated Pauli $P$ both at the root and in $v_P$. A Gaussian seed gate has the additional update $u\\mapsto e^{-\\mathrm itZs}u$.*\n\n*All these assertions are unchanged when the top root has $D+1$ children and the descendants have $D$ children, still using the coefficient $D^{-1/2}$. For an ordinary word $W$, its limiting insertion $v_Z$ therefore satisfies $v_W=\\mathcal E(v_Z)$, with $\\mathcal E$ defined in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:energy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:energy\">4</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-updates",

For a cost conjugated by a global mixer, replace $Z$ by the same rotated Pauli $P$ both at the root and in $v_P$. A Gaussian seed gate has the additional update $u\\mapsto e^{-\\mathrm itZs}u$.*\n\n*All these assertions are unchanged when the top root has $D+1$ children and the descendants have $D$ children, still using the coefficient $D^{-1/2}$. For an ordinary word $W$, its limiting insertion $v_Z$ therefore satisfies $v_W=\\mathcal E(v_Z)$, with $\\mathcal E$ defined in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:energy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:energy\">4</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-commuting-substitution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S_D=(S_{D,1},\\ldots,S_{D,b})$ be a fixed finite list of strongly commuting self-adjoint operators. Suppose their polynomials converge smoothly and their joint exponentials satisfy, for each $m$, bounds $$\\label{eq:joint-exponential-bound}\n \\|W_D^m e^{\\mathrm it\\cdot S_D}\\xi\\|\n \\leq C_m(1+|t|)^{k_m}\\|W_D^{r_m}\\xi\\|,$$ uniformly in $D,t$, and converge strongly on smooth inputs. Let $F_D(x)$ and $F_D(x)^*$ have uniform smooth bounds and converge with all their jets on compact $x$-sets, and suppose $F_D(x)$ commutes with the spectral projections of every $S_{D,j}$. Then the substitution $F_D(S_D)$ has uniform smooth bounds and converges smoothly. The assertion includes extra scalar parameters and their jets. If $F_D(x)$ is unitary for real $x$, so is $F_D(S_D)$. The limiting list $S$ consists of the strongly commuting self-adjoint generators of the limiting joint unitary group. Their actions and polynomials on the smooth domain agree with the assumed polynomial limits.*\n\n*For a real smooth function $f(s,x)$ whose derivatives have polynomial growth in $x$, uniformly in $s$, the operators $e^{\\mathrm it f(s,S_D)}$ have polynomial smooth bounds in $t$. Polynomial growth in a Gaussian $s$ is also permitted with the seed-moment weight. In every seed-dependent assertion, the local seed multiplication algebra is required to strongly commute with every $S_{D,j}$ and with the operator coefficient families; thus $f(s,S_D)$ is a joint commuting substitution. No nondegeneracy assumption on the joint covariance of the $S_{D,j}$ is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },

Let $F_D(x)$ and $F_D(x)^*$ have uniform smooth bounds and converge with all their jets on compact $x$-sets, and suppose $F_D(x)$ commutes with the spectral projections of every $S_{D,j}$. Then the substitution $F_D(S_D)$ has uniform smooth bounds and converges smoothly. The assertion includes extra scalar parameters and their jets. Their actions and polynomials on the smooth domain agree with the assumed polynomial limits.*\n\n*For a real smooth function $f(s,x)$ whose derivatives have polynomial growth in $x$, uniformly in $s$, the operators $e^{\\mathrm it f(s,S_D)}$ have polynomial smooth bounds in $t$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-gate-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite sequence of exclusion gates, ordinary mixers and costs, and local seed gates. Its root interaction-picture maps, their adjoints, and their root insertion vectors have smooth limits under the occupation embeddings. They have uniform smooth bounds at every sufficient finite height. The same limit is obtained at a full root with $D+1$ children. Hence their centered insertion energies are given by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:energy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:energy\">4</a>.*\n\n*An exclusion gate may be replaced inside any such fixed sequence by a sequence of pointwise unitary matrices with the same parameter calculations, provided every fixed number of aggregate-input derivatives converges on compact sets and has uniform polynomial bounds. Convergence almost everywhere in the local seed, or convergence in seed measure followed by a subsequence, is sufficient for bounded measurable local-seed coefficients. The resulting root maps, adjoints, and insertion vectors converge smoothly, and their energies converge.*\n\n\n*If local-seed coefficients are uniformly bounded, these bounds hold conditionally on the root seed, with constants independent of its value, using $W_0=1$. Root maps commute with root-seed multiplication. Consequently, if a root column $c$ has conditional $m$th graph norm at most $C_m$, then $$\\label{eq:masked-column-bound}\n \\|W^m\\mathbf 1_Jc\\|\\leq C_m\\sqrt{\\mu(J)}.$$ The same statements hold for any further scalar boundary-input jets of a fixed exclusion calculation. They also hold, including adjoint jets, for a finite list of external unscaled parameters, locally uniformly in those parameters, when the matrix and signal hypotheses hold jointly for their mixed jets.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Convergence almost everywhere in the local seed, or convergence in seed measure followed by a subsequence, is sufficient for bounded measurable local-seed coefficients. The resulting root maps, adjoints, and insertion vectors converge smoothly, and their energies converge.*\n\n\n*If local-seed coefficients are uniformly bounded, these bounds hold conditionally on the root seed, with constants independent of its value, using $W_0=1$. Root maps commute with root-seed multiplication. Consequently, if a root column $c$ has conditional $m$th graph norm at most $C_m$, then $$\\label{eq:masked-column-bound}\n \\|W^m\\mathbf 1_Jc\\|\\leq C_m\\sqrt{\\mu(J)}.$$ The same statements hold for any further scalar boundary-input jets of a fixed exclusion calculation. They also hold, including adjoint jets, for a finite list of external unscaled parameters, locally uniformly in those parameters, when the matrix and signal hypotheses hold jointly for their mixed jets.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"

The resulting root maps, adjoints, and insertion vectors converge smoothly, and their energies converge.*\n\n\n*If local-seed coefficients are uniformly bounded, these bounds hold conditionally on the root seed, with constants independent of its value, using $W_0=1$. Root maps commute with root-seed multiplication. Consequently, if a root column $c$ has conditional $m$th graph norm at most $C_m$, then $$\\label{eq:masked-column-bound}\n \\|W^m\\mathbf 1_Jc\\|\\leq C_m\\sqrt{\\mu(J)}.$$ The same statements hold for any further scalar boundary-input jets of a fixed exclusion calculation. They also hold, including adjoint jets, for a finite list of external unscaled parameters, locally uniformly in those parameters, when the matrix and signal hypotheses hold jointly for their mixed jets.",
      "explanation": "Fixed exclusion-gate sequences have smooth limiting maps, adjoints and insertions. Admissible matrix approximations remain valid inside a fixed surrounding sequence, including conditional bounds on masked root columns.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-gate-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite sequence of exclusion gates, ordinary mixers and costs, and local seed gates.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-smooth-exclusion-gate-calculus",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-gate-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "An exclusion gate may be replaced inside any such fixed sequence by a sequence of pointwise unitary matrices with the same parameter calculations, provided every fixed number of aggregate-input derivatives converges on compact sets and has uniform polynomial bounds. Convergence almost everywhere in the local seed, or convergence in seed measure followed by a subsequence, is sufficient for bounded measurable local-seed coefficients. The resulting root maps, adjoints, and insertion vectors converge smoothly, and their energies converge.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-gate-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "The same statements hold for any further scalar boundary-input jets of a fixed exclusion calculation.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-additive-response",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a preceding finite sequence as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:gate-calculus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:gate-calculus\">14</a>. Suppose a temporary Hamiltonian is diagonal in a common collection of site axes, with coefficients given by an exclusion calculation. Require its local Hamiltonian matrices and every aggregate-input derivative to have polynomial bounds as in Definition <a href=\"#def:exclusion-gate\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:exclusion-gate\">13</a>. In a cut branch let $H_D(a)$ denote the sum of the Hamiltonian terms affected by scalar boundary input $a$, truncated beyond its finite influence distance and conjugated by the preceding subtree circuit. Assume explicitly that $$\\label{eq:additive-response-parity}\n                  H_D(-a)=\\mathcal{P} H_D(a)\\mathcal{P},\n                  \\qquad \\mathcal{P}\\boldsymbol\\Omega=\\boldsymbol\\Omega,$$ where $\\mathcal{P}$ is the joint spin/seed symmetry. The first two boundary derivatives have smooth limits $H_\\alpha=\\lim_D\\partial_\\alpha H_D(0)$ and $H_{\\alpha\\lambda}=\\lim_D\\partial_\\alpha\\partial_\\lambda H_D(0)$, and $H_\\alpha$ is centered. These limits concern the response derivatives; no limit for the extensive $H_D(0)$ is required. The change in its $D$ child branches when zero boundary input is replaced by $a/\\sqrt D$ is $$\\sum_{j=1}^D\n   \\bigl[H_D(a/\\sqrt D)-H_D(0)\\bigr]^{(j)}.$$ Its smooth limit is $$\\label{eq:additive-cut-response}\n \\sum_\\alpha a_\\alpha q(H_\\alpha\\boldsymbol\\Omega)\n +\\frac12\\sum_{\\alpha,\\lambda}a_\\alpha a_\\lambda\n                           \\langle H_{\\alpha\\lambda}\\rangle_0.$$ The correction is realized as the self-adjoint generator of the limiting time group of the child cut ratios; the display specifies its action on the smooth domain. We use the same realization after commuting substitution and for the correction generators below. The same formula applies recursively to lower cut responses and to their input jets. It is continuous with the smooth bounds of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:gate-calculus\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:gate-calculus\">14</a>, for a fixed finite list of channels. Commuting parameter operators may replace $a$ under the same exclusions.*\n\n\n*In particular, if a sequence of such correction generators $R_j$ satisfies $\\|R_j\\xi\\|\\leq\\delta_j\\|W^r\\xi\\|$ with $\\delta_j\\to0$, then $$\\label{eq:small-generator-error}\n \\|(e^{\\mathrm itR_j}-1)\\xi\\|\n       \\leq |t|\\delta_j\\|W^r\\xi\\|.$$ If their exponentials and adjoints also have uniform smooth bounds, these errors tend to zero in every fixed lower graph norm and may be inserted in a fixed surrounding sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix $T,N$ and the scalar data in <a href=\"#eq:scalar-euler\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-euler\">[eq:scalar-euler]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:scalar-coefficients\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-coefficients\">[eq:scalar-coefficients]</a>, with all $c_j$ finite and positive.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-classical-trigonometric-density",
        "exact_quote": "Let $m^*=\\pi(z)$, where $z$ is a finite descendant formula with Gaussian leaf seeds. Assume that every transmitted child column is odd, and that each local map producing such a column or the output is a jointly odd smooth function of its local seed and finite list of odd aggregate inputs, with polynomial-growth derivatives. Purely local intermediate operations may be composed into these maps before the lemma is applied. For every $\\eta>0$, there is an odd bounded angle formula $\\theta$ such that $$\\lVert \\sin(2\\theta)-m^*\\rVert<\\eta.\n \\label{eq:angle-density}$$ Every sent column in the formula for $\\theta$ can be chosen bounded and odd. Its pointwise maps can be chosen to be finite real trigonometric sums in their finite lists of odd inputs, with odd joint parity for odd outputs. Its local seed inputs can be chosen bounded and odd. All these functions have bounded derivatives of every order in their continuous arguments.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },

        {

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-classical-color-exclusion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a descendant formula in which every transmitted column and the output are given by jointly odd bounded smooth functions of the local odd seed and the odd aggregate inputs, with bounded first derivatives. Let its propagation depth be $h$. It has a finite realization by colored formulas such that every aggregation excludes the current color, all colors of the path ancestors, and all helper colors. If $f$ is its original output and $f^{\\mathrm{col}}$ its colored output, set to zero on helper roots, then $$\\lVert f^{\\mathrm{col}}-f\\rVert^2\\le C(\\alpha+p_H),\n \\label{eq:color-exclusion-bound}$$ where $C$ depends only on the fixed color-free formula, not on the number or individual probabilities of its nonhelper colors. Conditional bounds for the unchanged nonlinear pointwise functions and their derivatives in the original grouped aggregate coordinates are uniform over these colors. No bound independent of $M$ is asserted for the Euclidean derivative norm after flattening all source-color fields into separate coordinates.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-classical-near-optimum",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\varepsilon>0$ and every $\\eta_H>0$, there are a finite color partition with helper probability $0<p_H<\\eta_H$, a finite number of helper types, and finite bounded odd angle formulas $\\theta_c$ on the nonhelper colors such that the following hold. Each transmitted column is bounded and odd, every pointwise map may be chosen as a finite trigonometric sum in its odd inputs, and every aggregation excludes its current color, all path-ancestor colors, and all helpers. Conditional on the finite labels, all continuous derivatives of the pointwise maps are bounded. The output $$m^{\\mathrm{col}}\n  =\\sum_{c=1}^M\\mathbf 1_{\\{C=c\\}}\\sin(2\\theta_c)\n \\label{eq:final-classical-formula}$$ is centered, satisfies $|m^{\\mathrm{col}}|\\le1$, vanishes on helper roots, and obeys $$\\mathcal E(m^{\\mathrm{col}})\\ge P_*-\\varepsilon.\n \\label{eq:final-classical-value}$$ After the uncolored finite formula has been fixed, the total helper probability may be taken arbitrarily small, with all individual helper probabilities positive. All depths, coefficients, label laws, and functions in the conclusion are fixed independently of degree, system size, and disorder.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-classical-synthesis",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite collection of bounded smooth odd seed-only exclusion formulas of Definition <a href=\"#def:exclusion-formulas\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:exclusion-formulas\">19</a>, with disjoint positive-probability helper masks, one for each positive aggregation depth, absent from all their dependencies and with output angle zero on their union $H$, and every $\\eta>0$, there is a finite seeded circuit whose insertion vector $v$ satisfies $$\\lVert \\mathbf 1_{H^c}(v-v_{\\mathrm{cl}})\\rVert<\\eta,\n \\label{eq:synthesis-insertion}$$ where $v_{\\mathrm{cl}}$ is the insertion vector of <a href=\"#eq:ideal-classical-rotations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ideal-classical-rotations\">[eq:ideal-classical-rotations]</a>. In particular its value obeys $$\\lvert \\mathcal E(v)-\\mathcal E(m)\\rvert\\le 2\\eta+2\\sqrt{p_H}.\n \\label{eq:synthesis-energy}$$ The circuit and all its angles are chosen independently of the degree, system size, and disorder. Helper probabilities can be arbitrarily small but are fixed and positive when the construction is performed.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-helper-contraction",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a fixed finite sequence in the parity-preserving, root-seed-commuting gate-calculus class, containing finitely many propagations <a href=\"#eq:desired-propagation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:desired-propagation\">[eq:desired-propagation]</a>, possibly with different $I,J,f,P$, and otherwise fixed gates. All its gates and classical parameters avoid a common helper $K$. Start with its spins pinned at $X=1$. Replace every indicated propagation by <a href=\"#eq:helper-contraction-generator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:helper-contraction-generator\">[eq:helper-contraction-generator]</a>, and refine their sender partitions so that their largest part probability tends to zero. Then all insertion vectors off $K$ converge to those of the original sequence. The root maps and their adjoints have uniform conditional smooth bounds on the compressed space, and converge strongly there up to scalar phases depending only on the root label. The same estimates hold on cut subtrees. Responses through the fixed lower formulas for $f$ have the uniform jet estimates proved below; no convergence of arbitrary boundary jets in the growing list of partition-dependent top channels is asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fixed-field-exponentials",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q_j=q(d_j)$, $1\\le j\\le k$, commute, where $k$ is fixed and $\\lVert d_j\\rVert_a\\le C_a$ for every $a$. Let $F_s:\\mathbb R^k\\to\\mathbb R$ have polynomial bounds on every derivative, uniformly in the local seed $s$. For bounded $t$, the operators $\\exp(\\mathrm itF_s(Q))$, their adjoints, and their time derivatives are uniformly smooth. Auxiliary parameter jets obey the same conclusion when the weighted column jets and the joint derivatives of $F$ have the corresponding uniform polynomial bounds. The constants require no lower bound on the Gram matrix of the $d_j$’s. The same assertion holds with a fixed root control axis, by conditioning on its two eigenvalues.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fixed-field-exponentials",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Q_j=q(d_j)$, $1\\le j\\le k$, commute, where $k$ is fixed and $\\lVert d_j\\rVert_a\\le C_a$ for every $a$. For bounded $t$, the operators $\\exp(\\mathrm itF_s(Q))$, their adjoints, and their time derivatives are uniformly smooth. Auxiliary parameter jets obey the same conclusion when the weighted column jets and the joint derivatives of $F$ have the corresponding uniform polynomial bounds. The constants require no lower bound on the Gram matrix of the $d_j$’s. The same assertion holds with a fixed root control axis, by conditioning on its two eigenvalues.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Q_j=q(d_j)$, $1\\le j\\le k$, commute, where $k$ is fixed and $\\lVert d_j\\rVert_a\\le C_a$ for every $a$. Auxiliary parameter jets obey the same conclusion when the weighted column jets and the joint derivatives of $F$ have the corresponding uniform polynomial bounds.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-orthogonal-quadratics",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R_l$ be finitely many pairwise disjoint root masks, with $p_l=\\Pr(R_l)$. Suppose $d_l,e_l$ are supported on $R_l$ and are real-commuting, meaning that all fields $q(d_l),q(e_l)$ commute pairwise. Suppose also that for every $a$ $$\\lVert d_l\\rVert_a+\\lVert e_l\\rVert_a\\le C_a\\sqrt{p_l}.$$ Set $$Q=\\sum_l\\left(q(d_l)q(e_l)-\\langle d_l,e_l\\rangle\\right),\n \\qquad \\rho=\\left(\\sum_l p_l^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Then $Q=O_{\\mathrm{sm}}(\\rho)$. Its exponentials and adjoints are uniformly smooth for bounded times, independently of the number of columns. They converge to the identity strongly on smooth vectors as $\\rho\\to0$, and in every fixed lower smooth norm on families with all higher smooth norms bounded.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-orthogonal-quadratics",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R_l$ be finitely many pairwise disjoint root masks, with $p_l=\\Pr(R_l)$. Suppose also that for every $a$ $$\\lVert d_l\\rVert_a+\\lVert e_l\\rVert_a\\le C_a\\sqrt{p_l}.$$ Set $$Q=\\sum_l\\left(q(d_l)q(e_l)-\\langle d_l,e_l\\rangle\\right),\n \\qquad \\rho=\\left(\\sum_l p_l^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$ Then $Q=O_{\\mathrm{sm}}(\\rho)$. Its exponentials and adjoints are uniformly smooth for bounded times, independently of the number of columns. They converge to the identity strongly on smooth vectors as $\\rho\\to0$, and in every fixed lower smooth norm on families with all higher smooth norms bounded.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $R_l$ be finitely many pairwise disjoint root masks, with $p_l=\\Pr(R_l)$.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-seed-simulation",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite seeded word and every $\\epsilon>0$, there is a finite ordinary cost–mixer word whose limiting tree energy differs from that of the seeded word by less than $\\epsilon$.         }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-seed-packet-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "The following estimates are uniform for bounded families of tails.*\n\n1.  *Displacements with bounded directions preserve all the seminorms $\\lVert \\cdot\\rVert_k$, with bounds depending only on the direction norm and $k$. For $\\lVert d\\rVert\\leq C$, $$W(rd)\\eta=\\eta+\\mathrm ir q(d)\\eta+O(r^2)\\lVert \\eta\\rVert_1\n     \\quad\\hbox{in norm}.$$ If $d_r-e_r\\to0$ in norm and both directions are bounded, then $(W(d_r)-W(e_r))\\eta_r\\to0$ in norm. These assertions also hold for $W_P$.*\n\n\n2.  *If $\\lVert d_r-e_r\\rVert\\geq\\kappa>0$, then for every integer $M$, $$\\label{eq:seed-packet-separation}\n     \\lvert \\langle W(d_r/r)\\eta_r,W(e_r/r)\\xi_r\\rangle\\rvert=O(r^M).$$ The conclusion is unchanged by inserting a bounded root spin matrix.*\n\n\n3.  *For bounded $d_r,v_r$ and fixed $\\tau$, conjugating the short probe $e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)}$ through a packet gives $$\\label{eq:seed-packet-probe}\n     W(d_r/r)^*e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)}W(d_r/r)\n     =e^{-\\mathrm i\\tau a_rP}e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)},\n     \\qquad a_r=-2\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\langle v_r,d_r\\rangle.$$ Consequently a fixed finite list of short probes preserves its scaled centers and bounded centered-tail moments, without any smallness assumption on $a_r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-seed-packet-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "The following estimates are uniform for bounded families of tails.*\n\n1.  *Displacements with bounded directions preserve all the seminorms $\\lVert \\cdot\\rVert_k$, with bounds depending only on the direction norm and $k$. These assertions also hold for $W_P$.*\n\n\n2.  *If $\\lVert d_r-e_r\\rVert\\geq\\kappa>0$, then for every integer $M$, $$\\label{eq:seed-packet-separation}\n     \\lvert \\langle W(d_r/r)\\eta_r,W(e_r/r)\\xi_r\\rangle\\rvert=O(r^M).$$ The conclusion is unchanged by inserting a bounded root spin matrix.*\n\n\n3.  *For bounded $d_r,v_r$ and fixed $\\tau$, conjugating the short probe $e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)}$ through a packet gives $$\\label{eq:seed-packet-probe}\n     W(d_r/r)^*e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)}W(d_r/r)\n     =e^{-\\mathrm i\\tau a_rP}e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)},\n     \\qquad a_r=-2\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\langle v_r,d_r\\rangle.$$ Consequently a fixed finite list of short probes preserves its scaled centers and bounded centered-tail moments, without any smallness assumption on $a_r$.",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The following estimates are uniform for bounded families of tails.*\n\n1.  *Displacements with bounded directions preserve all the seminorms $\\lVert \\cdot\\rVert_k$, with bounds depending only on the direction norm and $k$. These assertions also hold for $W_P$.*\n\n\n2.  *If $\\lVert d_r-e_r\\rVert\\geq\\kappa>0$, then for every integer $M$, $$\\label{eq:seed-packet-separation}\n     \\lvert \\langle W(d_r/r)\\eta_r,W(e_r/r)\\xi_r\\rangle\\rvert=O(r^M).$$ The conclusion is unchanged by inserting a bounded root spin matrix.*\n\n\n3.  *For bounded $d_r,v_r$ and fixed $\\tau$, conjugating the short probe $e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)}$ through a packet gives $$\\label{eq:seed-packet-probe}\n     W(d_r/r)^*e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)}W(d_r/r)\n     =e^{-\\mathrm i\\tau a_rP}e^{-\\mathrm ir\\tau Pq(v_r)},\n     \\qquad a_r=-2\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\langle v_r,d_r\\rangle.$$ Consequently a fixed finite list of short probes preserves its scaled centers and bounded centered-tail moments, without any smallness assumption on $a_r$.",
      "explanation": "Bounded displacements preserve tail moments, separated packets have rapidly vanishing overlap, and short probes retain packet centers while producing spin rotations. Norm remainders are handled separately from moment-controlled tails.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-seed-high-directions",
        "exact_quote": "The vectors $\\ell_r,w_r$ are centered, have norm one, and satisfy $$\\langle \\ell_r,w_r\\rangle\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathbf 1_{N\\leq M}\\ell_r\\rVert+\n \\lVert \\mathbf 1_{N\\leq M}w_r\\rVert\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad\\hbox{for every fixed }M.$$ They consequently have vanishing inner products with every bounded family of top-number-smooth vectors.",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-seed-ensemble-control",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h_1,\\ldots,h_m$ be a finite list of nonzero vectors in $\\mathbb R^2$. There are two perpendicular axes $P_1,P_2$ in the $ZY$-plane with the following property. Set $c_{j,a}=h_j\\cdot P_a$, using the coordinates $(Z,Y)$. Given any $\\theta\\in\\mathbb R$, there is a finite list $(a_k,\\tau_k)$, with $a_k\\in\\{1,2\\}$, such that $$\\label{eq:seed-zero-clocks}\n \\sum_{k:a_k=a}\\tau_k=0\\quad(a=1,2),\n \\qquad\n \\prod_k e^{-\\mathrm i\\tau_k c_{j,a_k}P_{a_k}}\n =e^{-\\mathrm i\\theta X}\\quad(1\\leq j\\leq m).$$ The product is in chronological order, with later factors on the left. The assertion is exact, not merely an assertion about closure.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-seed-selective-accuracy",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a stage whose vacuum state $u_r\\boldsymbol\\Omega$ has a finite packet expansion, with norm-$o(r)$ remainders and uniformly bounded scaled centers. Suppose the ordinary component has order-one amplitude and the special component has amplitude $O(r)$. All designated tails have bounded top-number moments. Suppose also that one of the following ordinary geometries holds.*\n\n1.  *At a standard stage all ordinary centers are zero.*\n\n2.  *At a huge-cost stage the ordinary part is $\\psi_++\\psi_-$, with $Z\\psi_y=y\\psi_y$ and centers $-tyv_r$, where $t\\neq0$ and $v_r=u_r^*Zu_r\\boldsymbol\\Omega$. Every special center $d_{j,r}$ obeys $\\lim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\langle v_r,d_{j,r}\\rangle\\neq0$.*\n\n*Assume the limiting special projection pairs $$h_j=\\lim_{r\\downarrow0}-2\n \\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\langle v_{Z,r},d_{j,r}\\rangle,\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\langle v_{Y,r},d_{j,r}\\rangle\\bigr)$$ exist and are nonzero. Use the axes and finite pulse list of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:seed-ensemble-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:seed-ensemble-control\">29</a> for these pairs, replacing its spin generators by actual toggled probes of angles $r\\tau_k$. Then the net probe action is the identity up to $o(r)$ on every ordinary packet, and is the prescribed $X$-rotation up to $o(1)$ on every normalized special packet. The ordinary assertion holds also after any root spin matrix has been inserted in its tail. These statements hold uniformly for bounded families of tails sharing the stated center list, with the probes chosen solely from the vacuum data.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-seed-one-echo",
        "exact_quote": "Under the invariant and the visibility conditions, the echo <a href=\"#eq:seed-echo-list\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:seed-echo-list\">[eq:seed-echo-list]</a> preserves the invariant.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-temperature-bound",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\beta_0>1$ and $0<T<q$, $$\\int_0^T\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt\n \\ge P_* -\\frac{1/4+\\log2}{\\beta_0}-(q-T).\n \\label{eq:positive-temperature-bound}$$ Moreover, $$\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)^2=1\\quad(0\\le t\\le q),\n \\qquad 1-q\\le\\beta_0^{-1}.\n \\label{eq:parisi-normalization-and-endpoint}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-281.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-temperature-bound",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\beta_0>1$ and $0<T<q$, $$\\int_0^T\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)\\,\\mathrm dt\n \\ge P_* -\\frac{1/4+\\log2}{\\beta_0}-(q-T).\n \\label{eq:positive-temperature-bound}$$ Moreover, $$\\mathbb Ea(t,X_t)^2=1\\quad(0\\le t\\le q),\n \\qquad 1-q\\le\\beta_0^{-1}.\n \\label{eq:parisi-normalization-and-endpoint}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-281.1"

        "exact_quote": "Consider a fixed finite descendant formula whose pointwise functions are smooth with all derivatives of polynomial growth. Assume its leaf seeds have moments of every order and every aggregated child column has mean zero under its finite-degree child law for each $D$. Its finite-degree root vector converges in norm, under the symmetric occupation embeddings, to the formula obtained by replacing every normalized sum by $\\mathsf G$. The same conclusion holds for a convergent finite collection of deterministic coefficients in the formula. The finite-degree root vectors have a uniformly bounded first child-occupation moment. Consequently, for centered equivariant outputs $f_D$ and their limit $f$, $$\\lim_{D\\to\\infty}\\frac{D+1}{2\\sqrt D}\\mathbb Ef_D(o)f_D(v)\n       =\\mathcal E(f),\\qquad o\\sim v.\n \\label{eq:classical-energy-transfer}$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finite descendant formula** ([source](#stmt-def-finite-descendant-formula)): A finite descendant formula is a finite expression built recursively from a local seed and finitely many Gaussian positions of centered formulas one level below, using pointwise scalar functions.
- **Exclusion calculation and gate** ([source](#stmt-def-exclusion-gate)): A finite exclusion calculation uses local seeds, one prescribed commuting spin axis at each control site, smooth pointwise functions of finite lists of messages, and normalized sums <a href="#eq:masked-adjacency" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:masked-adjacency">[eq:masked-adjacency]</a>.
- **Excluded aggregation** ([source](#stmt-def-exclusion-formulas)): An aggregation from $S$ to $R$ is *excluded* if the formula sent from $S$ uses no vertex in $R$ anywhere below that aggregation.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-thermodynamic-depth-limit | Limiting optimized QAOA expected energy per spin | = | $P_*$ | Deterministic cost and mixer angles are fixed independently of system size and disorder for each accuracy. |
| q-regular-maxcut-cut-fraction | Expected fraction of edges cut | ≥ | $\frac12+\frac{P_*-\eta}{\sqrt D}$ | Normalized angles are fixed; usual MaxCut cost angles are scaled with degree. |
| q-root-energy-continuity | Absolute difference of rooted energies | ≤ | $(\\|v\\|+\\|w\\|)\\|v-w\\|$ | The root shift is the fixed isometry defined in the rooted calculus. |
| q-color-exclusion-error | Squared norm error from color exclusions and helper deletion | ≤ | $C(\alpha+p_H)$ | The constant depends on the fixed color-free formula, independently of the number and individual probabilities of nonhelper colors. |
| q-seeded-synthesis-energy-error | Absolute energy difference between synthesized insertion and classical output | ≤ | $2\eta+2\sqrt{p_H}$ | Helper probabilities are positive and fixed during compilation; all circuit angles are deterministic. |
| q-positive-temperature-curvature-value | Integrated expected positive-temperature curvature | ≥ | $P_* -\frac{1/4+\log2}{\beta_0}-(q-T)$ | Temperature is finite and fixed; the stopping time lies below the support endpoint. |
| q-positive-temperature-support-endpoint | Distance of the positive-temperature support endpoint from the terminal time | ≤ | $\beta_0^{-1}$ | The support endpoint belongs to the fixed inverse temperature. |
| q-finite-step-output-variance | Second moment of the Gaussian finite-step output | = | $L\delta$ | Mesh and horizon are fixed in the degree limit; coefficient normalization is the stated martingale normalization. |
| q-euler-state-mean-square-error | Maximum mean-square difference between Euler states and diffusion states | ≤ | $C\delta$ | The constant depends on temperature and cutoff; no uniform terminal or temperature limit is claimed. |
| q-martingale-output-mean-square-error | Mean-square error of the Gaussian finite-step martingale output | ≤ | $C\delta$ | The comparison is made at fixed temperature and stopping time. |
| q-value-riemann-error | Absolute error between the finite-step energy sum and the integrated diffusion curvature | ≤ | $C\sqrt\delta$ | Degree has already tended to infinity at the fixed discretization; temperature and cutoff stay fixed while the mesh shrinks. |
