The cutoffs preserve continuity in the field variables, and uniform higher moments make the discarded squared norms uniformly small. This proves the induction step. It also proves continuity in deterministic coefficients, by applying the same argument to a convergent sequence of them.

For completeness, the occupation bound is not a consequence of the CLT alone. At a parent, let $P_j$ average over the $j$th child, and write its output as $H_D(s,S_{D,1},\ldots,S_{D,k})$, where $S_{D,l}=D^{-1/2}\sum_j f_{D,l}^{(j)}$. The occupation operator in the symmetric embedding is $N_D=\sum_{j=1}^D(I-P_j)$. With an independent replacement of child $j$, conditional variance and the mean-value theorem give $$\lVert (I-P_j)H_D\rVert^2
 \le\mathbb E|H_D-H_D^{(j)}|^2\le\frac{C}{D}.$$ Here the derivative of $H_D$ has polynomial growth and the sums, the replaced child columns, and their products have the uniform moments established above; Hölder’s inequality therefore gives a constant independent of $j,D$. Summing proves $\langle H_D,N_DH_D\rangle\le C$. If needed, the same argument with distinct child replacements and mixed derivatives bounds the factorial moments of $N_D$ of every fixed order.

The energy identity is now Proposition <a href="#prop:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:energy">4</a> applied to these classical vectors. More explicitly, with $M=D+1$ root children, the projection onto an excitation in a distinguished child has norm at most $C/\sqrt M$ by symmetry and the occupation estimate. The term with excitations across the distinguished edge on both sides is therefore $O(M^{-1})$. The two terms with only one crossing select a single child; after multiplication by $\sqrt M$ they converge to $\langle f,\mathsf Lf\rangle$ and its conjugate. The term with neither crossing is zero by centering. The factor $M/(2\sqrt D)$ in <a href="#eq:classical-energy-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:classical-energy-transfer">[eq:classical-energy-transfer]</a> thus gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\langle f,\mathsf Lf\rangle$, since $M/D\to1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:classical-eams-vector"></a>
Apply this lemma to the EAMS formulas at fixed $\delta,L$. All sent columns are odd under simultaneous reversal of the seed labels, since $a$ is even and $b$ is odd. They are therefore centered under each finite-degree child law, as required by the lemma. The smooth polynomial-growth hypotheses hold because a transmitted column is a bounded smooth coefficient times a message variable. Equation <a href="#eq:eams-normalizer-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:eams-normalizer-limit">[eq:eams-normalizer-limit]</a> and the coefficient-continuity assertion allow us to replace $c_{D,r}$ by the fixed limiting constants $c_{\delta,r}$ in the limiting formula. Let its output vector be $z^\delta$. We obtain $$\mathcal E(z^\delta)=\delta\sum_{r=1}^{L-1}
                    c_{\delta,r}\mathbb Ea(t_r,X^\delta_r),
 \quad
 \lVert z^\delta\rVert^2=L\delta,
 \quad
 z^\delta\ \hbox{has law }Z^\delta_L.
 \label{eq:classical-eams-vector}$$ All coefficients in this vector are independent of degree.

<a id="pm-h-51"></a>
#### Clipping and finite bounded formulas

<a id="eq:clipped-classical-vector"></a>
Let $\pi(x)=\max(-1,\min(x,1))$. Since $|m(T,X_T)|\le1$, the law in <a href="#eq:classical-eams-vector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:classical-eams-vector">[eq:classical-eams-vector]</a> and the metric projection property give $$\lVert z^\delta-\pi(z^\delta)\rVert^2
 =\mathbb E|Z^\delta_L-\pi(Z^\delta_L)|^2
 \le\mathbb E|Z^\delta_L-m(T,X_T)|^2.$$ Only the marginal law of $Z^\delta_L$ enters the equality. The diffusion coupling bounds this scalar clipping error; the energy of $z^\delta$ has already been identified separately in the rooted space by <a href="#eq:classical-eams-vector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:classical-eams-vector">[eq:classical-eams-vector]</a>. Consequently, by Lemma <a href="#lem:eams-positive-continuum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:eams-positive-continuum">38</a>, $\lVert z^\delta-\pi(z^\delta)\rVert\le C\sqrt\delta$. We apply the shared energy-continuity estimate <a href="#eq:energy-l2-continuity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-l2-continuity">[eq:energy-l2-continuity]</a> with $f=z^\delta$ and $g=\pi(z^\delta)$; both norms are at most one. Combining <a href="#eq:positive-temperature-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-temperature-bound">[eq:positive-temperature-bound]</a>, <a href="#eq:value-riemann-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:value-riemann-bound">[eq:value-riemann-bound]</a>, and <a href="#eq:classical-eams-vector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:classical-eams-vector">[eq:classical-eams-vector]</a> proves the following: for every $\varepsilon>0$, first choosing a sufficiently large finite $\beta_0$, then $T<q$ sufficiently close to $q$, and finally a sufficiently small positive $\delta$, gives a centered odd classical vector $$m^*=\pi(z^\delta),\qquad |m^*|\le1,
 \qquad \mathcal E(m^*)\ge P_*-\varepsilon.
 \label{eq:clipped-classical-vector}$$ At this point the depth $L$ and the entire descendant calculation are finite and fixed.

The next proposition completes this route using only the common bounded-approximation and exclusion lemmas. It gives the same conclusion as Proposition <a href="#prop:classical-near-optimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:classical-near-optimum">21</a>, with an independent proof from the clipped vector just constructed.

<a id="stmt-prop-classical-near-optimum-positive-temperature"></a>
<div id="prop:classical-near-optimum-positive-temperature" class="proposition">

**Proposition 40** (The independent positive-temperature construction). *For every $\varepsilon>0$ and every $\eta_H>0$, there are a finite color partition with helper probability $0<p_H<\eta_H$, a finite number of helper types, and finite bounded odd angle formulas $\theta_c$ on the nonhelper colors such that the following hold. Each transmitted column is bounded and odd, every pointwise map may be chosen as a finite trigonometric sum in its odd inputs, and every aggregation excludes its current color, all path-ancestor colors, and all helpers. Conditional on the finite labels, all continuous derivatives of the pointwise maps are bounded. The output <a id="eq:positive-final-classical-formula"></a>$$m^{\mathrm{col}}
  =\sum_{c=1}^M\mathbf 1_{\{C=c\}}\sin(2\theta_c)
 \label{eq:positive-final-classical-formula}$$ is centered, satisfies $|m^{\mathrm{col}}|\le1$, vanishes on helper roots, and obeys <a id="eq:positive-final-classical-value"></a>$$\mathcal E(m^{\mathrm{col}})\ge P_*-\varepsilon.
 \label{eq:positive-final-classical-value}$$ After the uncolored finite formula has been fixed, the total helper probability may be taken arbitrarily small, with all individual helper probabilities positive. All depths, coefficients, label laws, and functions in the conclusion are fixed independently of degree, system size, and disorder.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-classical-near-optimum-positive-temperature"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-classical-near-optimum-positive-temperature</summary>

*Proof.* Use <a href="#eq:clipped-classical-vector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:clipped-classical-vector">[eq:clipped-classical-vector]</a> with error $\varepsilon/3$. Its underlying formula $z^\delta$ has odd transmitted columns and jointly odd smooth local maps with polynomial-growth derivatives, as checked after Lemma <a href="#lem:classical-strong-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:classical-strong-limit">39</a>. It therefore meets the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:classical-trigonometric-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:classical-trigonometric-density">18</a>. Apply that lemma and then Lemma <a href="#lem:classical-color-exclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:classical-color-exclusion">20</a> with the choices in the proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:classical-near-optimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:classical-near-optimum">21</a>: the first output error is less than $\varepsilon/12$ in norm, the second less than $\varepsilon/6$, and one positive-probability helper is reserved per propagation depth with total probability below $\eta_H$. The two further energy losses are less than $\varepsilon/6$ and $\varepsilon/3$, respectively, by <a href="#eq:energy-l2-continuity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-l2-continuity">[eq:energy-l2-continuity]</a>. Their sum with the initial loss is less than $\varepsilon$. Those two shared lemmas also give the boundedness, trigonometric form, exclusions, and parity asserted here. The entire formula and all helper probabilities are fixed after the degree limit of the finite calculation. ◻

</details>
